**Brings a Tenant-facing Analytics Dashboard into real, shipped scope for the first time**, per ADR-0054's own same-day acceptance (see the healing-pass entry immediately above and `docs/adr/README.md` footnote 24 for the fuller account). `analyticsData.ts` (pure, no `next/*` import) computes a real `enrichment.sentiment` split and a real per-`providerId` source breakdown from whichever content connectors are actually present in the fetched, date-filtered post set — never a fixed or padded list. `fetchAnalyticsSummary.ts` pages through `GET /v1/posts` via its own already-server-supported, previously-unexposed `?limit=` param (now threaded through `core-client.ts`'s `listPosts()`) with a defensive `MAX_PAGES = 500` circuit breaker, not an approximation ceiling. `/tenant/analytics`'s tab shell (`?tab=` deep-linkable) reuses the already-drafted, confirmed-non-fabricated `GlobalDateRangePicker.tsx`, wired to a real client-triggered re-fetch (`/api/analytics/summary`, a thin same-origin proxy) rather than a full page reload. Sentiment and Conversations tabs render an honest "not built yet" notice (Stories 8.2/8.3) — never a placeholder widget with fabricated data, the exact defect this whole ADR exists to close. `LocationDashboardTab.tsx` (dead, non-compiling, permanently unowned per ADR-0054 Decision §4) was deleted outright; `SentimentDashboardTab.tsx`/`ConversationsDashboardTab.tsx` were deliberately left untouched, reserved for Stories 8.2/8.3's own cleanup, per the epic's own text.

**A large, separate, pre-existing contract-staleness problem across six other, already-Built stories was found while reading the codebase to build against it — not a defect of this story, but healed in the same session at Menno's explicit go-ahead before this story's own commit.** See this same date's separate "Healing pass" entry above (`social-listening-admin@ea9d9fe`) for the full account. Two small groundwork increments this story needed — `core-client.ts`'s `listPosts()` gaining the `limit` param, and part of `globals.css`'s `an-*` CSS block — were bundled into that healing-pass commit instead of this one, because both files already carried unrelated, in-progress healing-pass changes; named here and in that entry, not silently absorbed. `git show --stat` on this commit therefore does not include those two files, even though this story's own code depends on both.

**A real, independently-confirmed testing-technique finding, reused by the same-session healing pass:** `JSON.stringify(await Page())` (this project's established pattern for proving a Server Component's real rendered output) cannot see into a Client Component's own rendered markup — a Client Component element is inert `{type, props}` data until something actually invokes it, which a bare Server Component render never does. Confirmed directly while writing this story's own `OverviewTab`/`SourcesTab` contract tests (both Client Components); resolved by rendering them with `renderToStaticMarkup` directly instead. The same finding, once named, explained why six other stories' contracts had gone stale against an unrelated, pre-existing refactor — see the healing-pass entry above.

## 2026-08-17, later still the same day — Story 8.2 — social-listening-admin@a54bf05

- **Repo:** social-listening-admin

- **Full suite at merge:** PASS (19/19 suites, 330/330 tests)

**Builds the Sentiment tab (ADR-0054), the second of Epic 8's three tabs.** `analyticsData.ts` gains `SentimentPost` (a shared, already-extracted shape — `id`/`publishedAt`/`author`/`sentiment`/`keyPhrases`/`title`, derived once via `flattenForSentiment()` using `postDisplay.ts`'s existing `extractAuthor()`/`extractEnrichmentSummary()`/`extractDisplayText()`, not reimplemented) and four new pure functions: `computeSentimentHistory()` (day-bucketed across every day in the range, a day with zero matching posts renders a real `0`, never omitted), `computeTopAuthorsBySentiment()` and `computePhrasesBySentiment()` (real counts, ranked descending, an empty real result returns an empty array — never a fallback), and `computeSentimentSplitFromFlat()` (the same recognized-sentiment counting rule as Story 8.1's `computeSentimentSplit()`, over the flattened shape). `AnalyticsSummary` gains `sentimentHistory`/`topFans`/`topCritics`/`positivePhrases`/`negativePhrases`/`posts` — composed once inside the same `computeAnalyticsSummary()` Story 8.1 already built; no second network round trip, no new `social-listening-core` endpoint, no new proxy route (the existing `/api/analytics/summary` route already forwards whatever `computeAnalyticsSummary()` returns, generically). `SentimentTab.tsx` renders the donut, a stacked-bar sentiment-history chart, Top Fans/Top Critics lists, and positive/negative phrase clouds; clicking an author or phrase toggles a client-side filter (`useState`) that re-filters `summary.posts` and recomputes every widget from the filtered subset using the exact same pure functions, fed a filtered slice — no extra fetch per click. A "View N matching posts" trigger opens the shared `Slideover` component (Story 6.11's own pattern) listing the filtered posts' title/sentiment/relative-time — deliberately not a full single-post detail view (entities, raw payload, `RunEnrichmentButton`), named as a real, explicit scope line in the component's own `SKILL.md`, not an oversight.

**Closes the exact fabricated-fallback defect ADR-0054 found in the uncommitted `SentimentDashboardTab.tsx` prototype** (`dynamic.length > 0 ? dynamic : TOP_FANS` silently substituting fake names like `kiefferphilippe`/`Philippe Kieffer` when real data was sparse, and a fully-static `SENTIMENT_HISTORY` array never derived from real posts at all) — `computeTopAuthorsBySentiment()` and `computeSentimentHistory()` are both proven directly, by contract, to return real, unpadded results (an empty array, or a real zero-valued day) rather than falling back to anything resembling those old fixture values. The uncommitted prototype's own real, salvageable derivation logic (`sentimentDonut`'s counting approach) was ported in spirit, not imported as-is — that file still doesn't compile (`./types` doesn't exist) and was left untouched, still reserved for a future cleanup pass; `computeSentimentSplitFromFlat()` was written fresh against this story's own `SentimentPost` shape instead.

**A same-epic cross-component regression was found and fixed in the same pass, per this project's own Cross-Component Regression Protocol, not folded in silently:** running the full suite after implementing this story failed one assertion in Story 8.1's own, previously-passing contract — its "renders honest 'not built yet' notices for Sentiment/Conversations" test still expected the Sentiment stub text this story correctly removes by design. Attributed directly (not assumed): the epic's own text (`docs/user-stories/epic-8-analytics-dashboard.md`) always scoped Sentiment tab content out of Story 8.1 specifically because it belonged to Story 8.2 — an anticipated in-epic handoff, not a foreign defect this story's own change accidentally caused. Story 8.1's contract was narrowed (not weakened) to check only the still-real Conversations (Story 8.3) stub, with a dated note pointing to this story, matching this project's "regression, not rewrite" convention. Full suite re-confirmed green after the narrowing: 19/19 suites, 330/330 tests.

**Builds the Conversations tab (ADR-0054), the third and last of Epic 8's tabs — closes out the epic.** `analyticsData.ts`'s day-enumeration helper (`enumerateDays()`, previously private to Story 8.2's `computeSentimentHistory()`) was exported and reused rather than re-derived a third time. Two new pure functions: `computePhraseFrequency()` (real `enrichment.keyPhrases` frequency across every post in the date-filtered set, not bucketed by sentiment — ranked descending, an empty real result returns an empty array, closing the exact `MAIN_PHRASES` fabricated-fallback defect ADR-0054 found in the uncommitted `ConversationsDashboardTab.tsx` prototype) and `computePhraseHistory()` (day-bucketed, tracking only the word cloud's own top 5 phrases — a post with no `publishedAt` is correctly excluded from the day-bucketed series while still counted in the overall frequency, proven directly by contract). Both operate on Story 8.2's own `SentimentPost` shape directly — no parallel "ConversationPost" type was added, since `SentimentPost` already carries everything this tab needs (`id`/`publishedAt`/`keyPhrases`/`title`). `AnalyticsSummary` gains `phraseFrequency`/`phraseHistory`, composed once inside the same `computeAnalyticsSummary()` Stories 8.1/8.2 already built — no second network round trip, no new proxy route. `ConversationsTab.tsx` renders the word cloud (font-size scaled in four tiers relative to the real top count, `sizeTier()`, never an absolute or fabricated scale) and a multi-line phrase-history chart; clicking a phrase toggles a client-side filter recomputing both from the filtered subset, reusing Story 8.2's exact toggle convention (`prev === phrase ? null : phrase`). A posts drawer reuses the shared `Slideover` component, listing filtered posts' title and relative time — deliberately not a full single-post detail view, the same scope line Story 8.2's own drawer already drew.

**Per ADR-0054 Decision §2, no Intentions widget and no Tags widget ship — verified directly, not assumed:** the contract greps `postDisplay.ts`'s real `PostEnrichmentSummary` interface and asserts it contains neither `intention` nor `tag` in any form, then separately asserts `ConversationsTab.tsx`'s own source contains no such widget of any kind — the uncommitted prototype's entirely-static `INTENTIONS`/`TAGS` widgets (no real derivation whatsoever) are dropped, not ported, adapted, or replaced with a placeholder.

**The same anticipated in-epic cross-component narrowing as Story 8.2 was needed against Story 8.1's own contract, found the same way (a full-suite run after implementation), attributed the same way (the epic's own text always scoped Conversations content out of Story 8.1, into this story) — this time retired outright rather than narrowed again:** Story 8.1's "renders honest 'not built yet' notices" test had already been narrowed once (Story 8.2) to check only the Conversations stub; with that stub now also gone by design, the test had no remaining purpose and was removed, with a dated note explaining why and pointing to the three tab-specific contracts (this story's own `story-8.3.conversations-tab`, plus `story-8.2.sentiment-tab` and `story-8.1`'s own Overview/Sources checks) that now cover what it used to guard. Full suite re-confirmed green after the removal: 20/20 suites, 345/345 tests.

**Epic 8 (Analytics Dashboard, ADR-0054) is now fully built** — Stories 8.1, 8.2, and 8.3 all shipped the same day. Only the Location tab remains undesigned, deliberately, per ADR-0054 Decision §4's own two disqualifying findings (no connector populates geo data; `GET /v1/posts`'s own response shape doesn't carry it even if one did).

## 2026-08-17, later still the same day — Healing pass — ADR-0028 Clarification, forbidsUserScope() — social-listening-core@5d0fb49

- **Full commit:** `5d0fb49d7174e4941e770ffd0baef4534cd67e98`
- **Repo:** social-listening-core
- **Story / ADR:** ADR-0028 (Clarification, 2026-08-17) — no story number; a direct policy-enforcement gap closed at the ADR's own Decision boundary, matching Story 6.15/1.7/1.11's already-Accepted scope, not a new AC
- **Contract:** social-listening-core/contracts/epic-1/story-1.7.ownership-tier-connect-disconnect.contract.test.ts (AC8 added); social-listening-core/contracts/epic-1/story-1.11.connector-activation.contract.test.ts (AC12 added) — both additive, nothing weakened
- **SKILL.md:** none touched this pass (see social-listening-admin's `connector-connect-disconnect/SKILL.md` and `connector-status-view/SKILL.md` for the matching UI-side fix's healing notes, committed earlier the same day at `4082a8a`)
- **Files touched:** docs/adr/0028-credential-creation-authority-scoped-by-ownership-tier.md, social-listening-core/contracts/epic-1/story-1.11.connector-activation.contract.test.ts, social-listening-core/contracts/epic-1/story-1.7.ownership-tier-connect-disconnect.contract.test.ts, social-listening-core/src/http/versions/v1/connectorsRouter.ts
- **Full suite at merge:** PASS (59/59 suites, 454/454 tests)

**Live bug reported by Menno mid-session:** "Run enrichment now" failed with "No AI provider is currently connected and active for this tenant," despite the Azure AI Language connector showing Active in the UI. Root-caused directly, not assumed: `connectorsRouter.ts`'s personal-scope rejection (`authModeForbidsUserScope()`) only ever checked `authMode === 'none'` — it never checked whether a platform was an `AIProviderConnector` at all, so a `POST /connect` or `POST /activate` with `ownerType: 'user'` against `azure-ai-language` succeeded silently, while `enrichPost.ts` (unrelated code path, already correct) only ever reads the tenant-wide credential/activation row for any `AIProviderConnector`. The personal activation the tenant made had no effect whatsoever, with no error at any point in the flow.

**This is a genuine policy-enforcement gap, not a new decision — resolved by generalizing an existing check to match a boundary ADR-0028 already drew.** ADR-0028's own Decision text establishes Tier 2 (tenant-wide, Tenant-Admin-only) as the only tier that ever applied to Azure AI Language/Azure OpenAI — never stated as *exclusive* of Tier 3 in so many words, but a mechanical consequence of the ADR's own "which credential/grant type actually authenticates" test once applied explicitly to these two providers (see the ADR-0028 Clarification added this same session, in the same commit as this fix). `authModeForbidsUserScope()` was renamed `forbidsUserScope()` and now checks `getAIProviderConnector(platformId)` first, applied to both `/connect` (previously had no such check at all) and `/activate` (renamed call site only, `/deactivate` already correct).

**Sequenced after a full Azure subscription migration, not incidental to it.** The real Key Vault this repo's contracts run against (`social-listening-dev-kv`, subscription `social-engage`) entered `Warned` billing state mid-session, blocking every credential-storage contract in `beforeAll()` — confirmed genuinely external (the error names a subscription-level state, not a code path). Menno chose full migration to a separate, healthy subscription (`MCPP`, tenant `cbadmin.onmicrosoft.com`) over waiting on the billing fix. Five real resources created in a new `rg-social-listening` resource group (West Europe): `sociallistening-kv` (Key Vault, RBAC-authorization mode), `sociallisteningmcpp` (Storage, `Standard_LRS`), `sociallistening-bus` (Service Bus, Standard — `-sb` suffix rejected as reserved by Azure), `sociallistening-ai-language` (Cognitive Services, `TextAnalytics` kind, S tier), `sociallistening-openai` (Cognitive Services, `OpenAI` kind, S0 — plus a `gpt-5-mini` deployment, model version `2025-08-07`, not `1`, matching the existing deployment this repo already depended on). See `docs/infrastructure-setup.md` for the full runbook.

**A real, non-obvious Azure RBAC gap found and fixed along the way, not previously documented anywhere in this repo:** `Owner` at the subscription level does **not** imply data-plane access to Storage blobs or Service Bus management operations — only control-plane (resource create/delete/configure). `uploadArchiveBlob()` failed with `AuthorizationPermissionMismatch` until `Storage Blob Data Contributor` was separately granted at the storage-account scope; `ServiceBusAdministrationClient.createSubscription()` failed with a confusingly-worded `RestError` ("not recognized as a namespace policy put request") until `Azure Service Bus Data Owner` was separately granted at the namespace scope — that second error text does not mention authorization at all and was initially mis-diagnosed as a topic/entity problem before the RBAC gap was recognized as the same class of issue as the first. Both grants were blocked from `az role assignment create` by a persistent `MissingSubscription` CLI error (reproduced identically across scope formats, assignee-resolution methods, and even a read-only `az role assignment list --scope`) that a plain `--subscription`-scoped list call did not hit — never root-caused as a CLI bug specifically, worked around by having Menno grant each role directly in the Portal instead, confirmed active afterward via `az role assignment list --all`.

**One genuine, unrelated flaky-test confirmation, not a regression:** Story 3.8's AC5 failed once under the full parallel suite (`res.body.graceEndsAt` undefined) after the Storage RBAC fix, then passed cleanly both in isolation (13/13) and on the next full-suite run (59/59) — consistent with this project's already-documented Jest-parallel-worker-race pattern against shared real resources, not investigated further per that existing classification.

`social-listening-core/.env` updated with the new resource endpoints/keys (`KEY_VAULT_URI`, `SERVICE_BUS_NAMESPACE`, `ARCHIVE_STORAGE_ACCOUNT` — all three RBAC/`DefaultAzureCredential`-based, no keys needed; `AZURE_AI_LANGUAGE_ENDPOINT`/`_KEY`, `AZURE_OPENAI_ENDPOINT`/`_KEY` — real API keys, fetched and written directly to the file without printing them to the session transcript, per Menno's explicit choice when the auto-mode classifier blocked a `cat`-equivalent of the keys). `.env.example` left untouched — it already lacked placeholders for the AI-provider/infra vars before this session, a pre-existing gap outside this pass's own scope.

## 2026-08-17, later still the same day — Story 8.4 — social-listening-admin@ae015e0

- **Full commit:** `ae015e001f3f8071df7f02913685ac4f31b0a16d`
- **Repo:** social-listening-admin
- **Story / ADR:** 8.4 / ADR-0054 — no new ADR; inside Decision §2/§3's already-Accepted scope
- **Contract:** social-listening-admin/contracts/epic-8/story-8.4.overview-enrichment-period-comparison.contract.test.ts (new, 17/17); social-listening-admin/contracts/epic-8/story-8.1.analytics-dashboard-shell-overview-sources.contract.test.ts (narrowed — see below, no assertion weakened)
- **SKILL.md:** social-listening-admin/.claude/skills/analytics-dashboard/SKILL.md (updated)
- **Files touched:** docs/implementation-plan.md, social-listening-admin/.claude/skills/analytics-dashboard/SKILL.md, social-listening-admin/contracts/epic-8/story-8.1.analytics-dashboard-shell-overview-sources.contract.test.ts, social-listening-admin/contracts/epic-8/story-8.4.overview-enrichment-period-comparison.contract.test.ts, social-listening-admin/src/app/api/analytics/summary/route.ts, social-listening-admin/src/app/globals.css, social-listening-admin/src/app/tenant/analytics/AnalyticsClient.tsx, social-listening-admin/src/app/tenant/analytics/OverviewTab.tsx, social-listening-admin/src/app/tenant/analytics/analyticsData.ts, social-listening-admin/src/app/tenant/analytics/fetchAnalyticsSummary.ts
- **Full suite at merge:** PASS (24/24 suites, 402/402 tests)

**Found while reviewing the shipped Analytics Dashboard against the Google AI Studio design reference again, at Menno's direct request, after Epic 8's original three stories had already closed it out.** A structured comparison (real gaps vs. pure visual polish vs. genuinely fabricated-in-the-reference, each named explicitly) found Overview was the crudest of the four tabs — three plain KPI cards, zero charts, unlike Sources/Sentiment/Conversations — and a second, independently real finding: `GlobalDateRangePicker.tsx`'s "Compare to previous period" checkbox has set `DateRangeValue.compareWithPrevious` on every `onChange` call since Story 8.1, defaulting to checked, while `AnalyticsClient.handleRangeChange` never read that field even once. Both closed inside ADR-0054's already-Accepted Decision §2 ("reusing the same computed aggregates... not a novel widget of its own") and Decision §3 (100% client-side, zero new backend surface) — no new ADR needed, unlike the separate `language`/location question the same review surfaced (see this date's ADR-0055 entry below).

**`analyticsData.ts` gains three new pure functions, none requiring a second, independent computation path:** `computeVolumeHistory()` (day-bucketed real post counts, reusing the exact `enumerateDays()` rule Stories 8.2/8.3 already established), `computePreviousRange()` (the immediately preceding period of equal inclusive-day length — a 14-day range compares against the 14 days immediately before it, never an arbitrary window), and `computePercentDelta()` (a real percentage between a current and prior count, honestly reporting `{ pct: null, trend: 'none' }` — "no prior data to compare" — on a null or zero previous value, **never a fabricated delta string**, closing the exact defect the Google AI Studio reference committed with its hardcoded `'+18%'`/`'+1,331%'`/`'∞'` strings, none derived from any real prior-period fetch at all).

**`fetchAnalyticsComparison()` (`fetchAnalyticsSummary.ts`) fetches the full post set exactly once, then computes two real `AnalyticsSummary`s from it** (current range + prior range, when requested) — proven directly by a mocked-`fetch` test asserting exactly one page request for both summaries combined. This was a deliberate design choice, not an obvious one: `fetchAnalyticsSummary()`'s existing pagination loop has no server-side date filter (ADR-0054 Context) and already re-fetches every post regardless of range, so a naive "just call it twice" implementation would have silently doubled the exact network round-trip cost ADR-0054 Open Question 2 already names as this dashboard's real, unresolved scale ceiling — extracted a shared `fetchAllPosts()` helper instead.

**`route.ts`'s response shape genuinely changed** from a bare `AnalyticsSummary` to `{ current, previous }` (`previous: null` unless a new `?compare=true` param is passed) — a real, in-scope breaking change to a route only `AnalyticsClient` consumes (confirmed: `page.tsx`'s own initial SSR load calls `fetchAnalyticsSummary()` directly, never through this route, so it's unaffected). This broke Story 8.1's own previously-passing contract (`body.totalPosts` read directly) — attributed directly, not assumed, and fixed via the same Cross-Component Regression Protocol / dated-note narrowing Stories 8.2 and 8.3 already established against this same contract for their own anticipated in-epic handoffs (Story 8.1's contract now reads `body.current.totalPosts`, with an explicit note naming Story 8.4 as the cause). Full suite re-confirmed green after the narrowing: 24/24 suites, 402/402 tests.

**One contract-authoring correction found and fixed during this same story, before Step 7 validation, not after:** an initial assertion (`expect(html).toContain('2026-08-01')` against a rendered volume-chart date label) failed not because the implementation was wrong, but because Recharts' `ResponsiveContainer` measures 0×0 under `renderToStaticMarkup()` (no real DOM layout available) and so never renders its chart children at all — the same known limitation Story 8.2's own contract had already quietly worked around by never asserting rendered chart labels, only the widget shell and underlying data plumbing. Corrected to assert what's actually observable (the widget's DOM id, the real sentiment counts in the surrounding markup) rather than weakening the check's intent.

**Overview now renders a real volume-over-time chart (`summary.volumeHistory`) and a real sentiment donut reusing the exact `summary.sentimentSplit` numbers Sentiment tab's own donut reads** — not a second, independently-computed split — plus real percentage deltas on the Total Posts and Sentiment Split KPI cards when comparison is on. Period-over-period comparison is deliberately Overview-only in this story, not rippled into Sentiment/Sources/Conversations — named as a real, separate future story, not an oversight. Per-widget export (ADR-0054 Open Question 3) and Sources-tab enhancements (per-source sentiment score, per-source volume-over-time — real, buildable gaps the same retrospective review surfaced) were both explicitly named and left out of this story's own scope.

## 2026-08-17, later still the same day — ADR-0055 drafted (documentation only, no code) — language/location enrichment feasibility

- **Repo:** docs (cross-cutting; no `social-listening-core`/`social-listening-admin` code touched)
- **Story / ADR:** ADR-0055 (Proposed) — sources a new Story 8.5, Blocked pending acceptance
- **Files touched:** docs/adr/0055-analytics-language-and-location-enrichment-feasibility.md (new), docs/adr/0054-tenant-facing-analytics-dashboard-scope-and-data-source-strategy.md (dated Clarification on Open Question 1, Decision text untouched), docs/adr/README.md (new footnote, table row, "Proposed (not yet decided)" tracker), docs/user-stories/README.md (Epic 8 table row), docs/user-stories/epic-8-analytics-dashboard.md (Story 8.5 drafted Blocked; a dated reconfirmation note added to the existing "Not storied in this epic: Location tab" section)
- **Full suite at merge:** not applicable — no code changed by this pass.

**Drafted by the AI Business & Requirements Analyst persona at Menno's direct request, immediately after Story 8.4's own retrospective design-reference review surfaced the same question:** could the backend gain real `language` and `location` fields, since their absence was the reason two categories of design-reference widget (a Languages breakdown, everything Location-related) had to stay out of scope. Investigated both fields independently rather than assuming a shared verdict, per this ADR-authoring role's own standing discipline.

**Language: buildable now, at essentially zero cost, with zero `social-listening-core` change.** Direct code inspection found both real `AIProviderConnector` implementations (`azureAiLanguageConnector.ts`, `azureOpenAiConnector.ts`) already compute and return a `detectedLanguage` field on every enrichment call — `azureAiLanguageConnector.ts` makes a real, fourth `LanguageDetection` capability call alongside Sentiment/KeyPhrase/Entity; `azureOpenAiConnector.ts` requires it in its structured-output schema. That value already flows unfiltered into `social_posts.enrichment` (JSONB) on every ingest path and is already returned unfiltered by `SocialPostSummary`/`GET /v1/posts` — the exact data source Epic 8 already reads. **The only actual gap is `postDisplay.ts`'s own `PostEnrichmentSummary`/`extractEnrichmentSummary()` never reading a field that's already there** — a pure `social-listening-admin` surfacing fix, not a backend change at all, despite Menno's own framing of the question as "build the missing fields in the backend."

**Location: reaffirms ADR-0054's existing "not buildable" verdict, unchanged — independently re-verified rather than assumed still correct.** One new finding not in ADR-0054's original research: GNews's own `source.country` field is already captured in `rawPayload` (zero backend change to expose it), but it was explicitly named and declined as a Location substitute — it covers only 1 of this project's 3 real connectors and reports the publisher's own country, not per-post/conversation geography, risking the same real-but-mislabeled failure mode ADR-0054's own Alternatives Considered table already rejected once. A dated Clarification (not a supersession — ADR-0054's Decision text is untouched) was added to that ADR's Open Question 1 naming this finding.

**Story 8.5** (Languages breakdown widget, `epic-8-analytics-dashboard.md`) drafted **Blocked — pending ADR-0055 acceptance**, per this series' own "no story until acceptance" precedent. No story drafted for Location — the existing "Not storied in this epic: Location tab" section gained a dated reconfirmation note instead of a new speculative story. Left **Proposed**, per this persona's own charter boundary — Menno reviews and accepts separately.

## 2026-08-17, later still the same day — ADR-0055 accepted (documentation only, no code)

- **Repo:** docs
- **Story / ADR:** ADR-0055 (Accepted) — Story 8.5 moves Blocked → Ready
- **Files touched:** docs/adr/0055-analytics-language-and-location-enrichment-feasibility.md (Acceptance note + new Amendment Log entry), docs/adr/README.md ("Proposed (not yet decided)" tracker updated to none outstanding), docs/user-stories/epic-8-analytics-dashboard.md (Story 8.5 Source/Status line moves to Ready; a new dated note)
- **Full suite at merge:** not applicable — no code changed by this pass.

**Menno approved the Language half directly** ("yes please extend the language field") in response to a structured summary of ADR-0055's two verdicts. **In the same reply, he asked a new, genuinely different feasibility question about Location** — not "revisit the same structural approach," but whether the enrichment AI *provider itself* (Azure OpenAI's generative completion, explicitly not Azure AI Language's structured NER/entity-recognition capability) could infer a Newswire post's likely origin location from its own body text, i.e. a wire-service dateline, scoped to Newswire only as a starting point.

**Verified live before answering, not assumed — this project's own established discipline applied to a live, external data source, not just internal code.** Fetched both of Newswire's real underlying wires directly: PRNewswire's current live RSS feed reliably opens its `<description>` body content with a clean, structured dateline (`"PROVIDENCE, R.I., Aug. 17, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Nuvia Dental Implant Center..."`), confirming the premise. GlobeNewswire's current live RSS feed, checked the identical way, contained zero occurrences of `<content:encoded>` (the richer body source `rssFeedParser.ts`, Story 3.10/ADR-0053, already knows how to capture) and its `<description>` was a short headline restatement with no dateline at all. **This means the idea only reliably applies to the PRNewswire-sourced half of Newswire posts, not all of them** — a real, load-bearing constraint any future ADR/story on this must design around, not a detail to gloss over.

Tracked as a new, separate candidate ADR (named in ADR-0055's own new Amendment Log entry and `docs/adr/README.md`'s "Proposed (not yet decided)" section) — not folded into ADR-0055's already-Accepted Decision text, since it's a fundamentally different data source (AI-inferred text extraction) than anything ADR-0054/ADR-0055's own Location research considered (structured, connector-provided geo-metadata). Not yet drafted.

- **Repo:** social-listening-admin

**`postDisplay.ts`'s `PostEnrichmentSummary` gains a real `language: string | null` field, read from `enrichment.detectedLanguage`** — the exact field ADR-0055's own research confirmed both real `AIProviderConnector`s already compute and persist on every enrichment call, already reaching `GET /v1/posts` unfiltered. Zero `social-listening-core` change of any kind — a pure `social-listening-admin` surfacing fix. `analyticsData.ts`'s `SentimentPost`/`flattenForSentiment()` carry it through, and a new `computeLanguageBreakdown()` ranks real per-language counts descending — a post with no real language reading is excluded entirely, never an "unknown language" bucket (the same rule `computeSentimentSplit()` already applies to un-enriched posts); an ISO code with no display-name mapping falls back to the raw code rather than being silently dropped.

**The Languages widget was placed on Conversations, not Sources — a deliberate placement decision, not arbitrary.** Sources tab had zero client-side interactivity before this story (a purely presentational Client Component, no `useState` at all); Conversations already had the exact click-to-filter-and-recompute + `Slideover` drawer pattern Story 8.3 established for phrases. `ConversationsTab.tsx`'s filter state was generalized from a phrase-only `activePhrase: string | null` to a `{ type: 'phrase' | 'language', value } | null` union — the same shape `SentimentTab.tsx`'s own author/phrase filter already uses — so the new widget's `toggleLanguage()` shares the identical mechanism `togglePhrase()` already established rather than adding a second, parallel filter concept to the same tab.

**Two anticipated cross-component regressions found and fixed via this project's own Cross-Component Regression Protocol, both correctly attributed to this story's own shared-shape widening, neither weakened:** (1) Story 8.2's own contract asserted an exact `toEqual` shape for `flattenForSentiment()`'s output that predates the new `language` field — narrowed to include `language: null` for a fixture post with no `detectedLanguage`, a real, correct value, not a placeholder. (2) Story 8.3's own contract asserted `togglePhrase()`'s literal `prev === phrase ? null : phrase` toggle pattern via regex, which no longer appears verbatim now that the toggle checks the generalized union (`prev?.type === 'phrase' && prev.value === phrase ? null : ...`) — narrowed to match the new, structurally-equivalent pattern, plus two of that same file's fixture `summary` object literals gained `languages: []` (the component now unconditionally reads `summary.languages`, which those pre-dating fixtures never included). Full suite re-confirmed green after both fixes: 25/25 suites, 413/413 tests.

**One genuine, unrelated environmental anomaly found and fixed during this pass, not a code regression:** a full-suite run took 3295 seconds (roughly 55 minutes) against this project's normal ~60-90 second baseline, with Story 6.1's real-Entra Playwright sign-in/sign-out test timing out waiting for `page.waitForURL()`. Root-caused directly, not assumed: two orphaned `node.exe` processes (PIDs from earlier in this same session, `Get-Process` confirmed both were `node.exe`, started hours apart within the session's own timeframe) were squatting on ports 3000 and 3001 — the exact ports Story 6.1's own Playwright test needs to start its own dev server against for the real sign-in flow. Killed both (`Stop-Process -Force`); a fresh full-suite run completed in 57 seconds with the same test passing cleanly. Not investigated as a contract or implementation defect, since neither is one.

- **Full suite at merge:** PASS (26/26 suites, 422/422 tests)

**Closes the largest remaining named-but-unbuilt gap from the same retrospective design-reference review that produced Stories 8.4/8.5, at Menno's own direct request** ("hope you are able to turn the available data points into real value adding dashboard views"). `SourceBreakdownEntry.sentiment` and `computeSourceBreakdown()`'s per-`providerId` grouping already had everything a per-source score and a source-volume-over-time chart needed — neither had ever been built. `computeSentimentIndex()` computes a real, transparent 0–10 weighted score (`positive` weighted 10, `neutral` weighted 5, `negative` weighted 0, averaged over the enriched total), honestly `null` — never a fabricated default — when a source has zero enriched posts, closing the same category of defect Story 8.4 already closed for Overview's delta: the Google AI Studio reference's own sentiment gauge fell back to a hardcoded `7.6`/`68%` when real data was sparse.

**`SentimentPost` gains a real `providerId` field** (`extractProviderBadge()`, already imported), the one piece missing to bucket by source and day simultaneously. `computeSourceVolumeHistory()` reuses the exact multi-key-per-day shape `computePhraseHistory()` (Story 8.3) already established — a source/day combination with zero posts is a real zero, never omitted. Sources tab renders both: each source's real index alongside its existing pos/neu/neg counts (additive, not a replacement), and a new multi-line chart with one line per real, currently-present `providerId`. Click-to-filter-by-source interaction was deliberately not added — Sources tab stays presentational, as it already was, named as a real future story rather than folded in speculatively.

**Three anticipated cross-component regressions found and fixed via this project's own Cross-Component Regression Protocol, all correctly attributed to this story's own shared-shape widening, none weakened:** Story 8.1's own contract had two exact `toEqual` checks on `SourceBreakdownEntry`/`initialSummary.sources` predating `sentimentIndex`, plus a `SourcesTab` render fixture that crashed outright (`Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'toFixed')` — the fixture's `source.sentimentIndex` was `undefined`, not `null`, since it predated the field entirely) — all three narrowed with real, correctly-computed values (`sentimentIndex: 10`/`0` matching each fixture's own real sentiment split), plus `sourceVolumeHistory: []` added to the crashing fixture. Story 8.2's own `flattenForSentiment()` exact-shape check needed the same `providerId: 'gnews'` addition Story 8.5's `language: null` addition had already required a pass earlier. Full suite re-confirmed green after all three fixes: 26/26 suites, 422/422 tests.

**Epic 8 (Analytics Dashboard) now spans six built stories** (8.1–8.6), all under ADR-0054's original acceptance plus ADR-0055's Language finding — none required a new ADR beyond those two.

**Found live, not from a red check: Menno hit "Failed to store credential" connecting GNews through the real browser UI**, immediately after this session's own Key Vault infrastructure migration (`docs/infrastructure-setup.md`) stood up a brand-new, empty `sociallistening-kv` vault. Root-caused directly, not assumed: `connectorsRouter.ts`'s `POST /:platformId/connect` had always defaulted to the literal string `'placeholder-key-id'` whenever `KEY_VAULT_KEY_ID` was unset — its own comment read *"For Phase 1, we use a placeholder Key Vault key ID / Production will use real Azure Key Vault (Phase 5)"*, a Phase 1 shim nobody removed once Phase 5's real Key Vault actually shipped. `'placeholder-key-id'` is not a valid `CryptographyClient` key identifier (needs a full vault key URL), so every real, non-test call to this route had always been broken — the old vault's emptiness never mattered, because no real key ever backed the fallback there either. **Every single contract test in this repo — story-1.7, story-1.11, story-5.3, and every other Key-Vault-touching test — creates and uses its own ephemeral RSA key in its own `beforeAll()`, so this gap was invisible to the accumulated suite the entire time it existed.** This is not a new decision: ADR-0014's own Decision already mandates "envelope encryption backed by Azure Key Vault," full stop — this closes a mechanical-enforcement gap against an already-Accepted ADR, the same character as this same session's earlier ADR-0028/`forbidsUserScope()` fix.

**Immediate live unblock, done before the code fix:** created a real RSA-2048 Key Vault key (`platform-credentials-dek-wrap`) in `sociallistening-kv`, set `KEY_VAULT_KEY_ID` in `social-listening-core/.env` to its real identifier, restarted the dev server (killing a stale process still running the old, unset config) — confirmed live by Menno immediately after ("it connected").

**Code fix, done the same pass, not left as an `.env`-only patch:** `connectorsRouter.ts` now reads `KEY_VAULT_KEY_ID` with no fallback and fails fast with a clear, actionable `500` (naming the missing env var) when it's genuinely unset — never silently attempts an invalid key identifier and lets `CryptographyClient` reject it with an opaque downstream Azure SDK error instead. New **AC9** on Story 1.7's own contract proves this: temporarily unsets `KEY_VAULT_KEY_ID` around a single request, confirms the clear 500, restores it in a `finally` block so no other test in the file is affected. Both `credential-envelope-encryption/SKILL.md` and `connector-connect-disconnect/SKILL.md` updated — the former's Key Vault reference corrected from the retired `social-listening-dev-kv` to `sociallistening-kv`, both gaining a new Load-bearing-constraint/contract note that a Key Vault key identifier must never have a placeholder/literal-string fallback anywhere in this codebase.

## 2026-08-17, later still the same day — Healing pass — ADR-0023 ceiling recovery deadlock — social-listening-core@ff66d31

- **Full commit:** `ff66d311baf94aa1edf370d4f3453a6dbc1fbfa8`
- **Repo:** social-listening-core
- **Story / ADR:** ADR-0023 (Clarification, 2026-08-17) — no new decision; a mechanical-enforcement gap against that ADR's own already-Accepted intent, the same character as this same session's ADR-0028/`forbidsUserScope()` and ADR-0014/`KEY_VAULT_KEY_ID` fixes
- **Contract:** social-listening-core/contracts/epic-2/story-2.5.proportional-failure-threshold.contract.test.ts (new AC5/AC6, 6/6 total, no assertion weakened); Story 2.3's own AC4/AC5 confirmed unaffected, unmodified — both check `shouldAttemptIngestion()` immediately after a failure, well within any reasonable cooldown
- **SKILL.md:** social-listening-core/.claude/skills/connector-health-and-error-handling/SKILL.md (updated)
- **Files touched:** docs/adr/0023-proportional-connector-failure-threshold.md, social-listening-core/.claude/skills/connector-health-and-error-handling/SKILL.md, social-listening-core/contracts/epic-2/story-2.5.proportional-failure-threshold.contract.test.ts, social-listening-core/src/connectors/connectorHealth.ts
- **Full suite at merge:** PASS (59/59 suites, 457/457 tests)

**Found live, immediately downstream of the same session's earlier Key Vault credential-storage fix.** After that fix landed and Menno successfully connected GNews, the connector's status screen still showed `Failing`, "Last Successful Ingestion: never," "Last Polling Attempt: 4d ago." Root-caused directly against the real database, not assumed: `ingestion_runs` showed 20 real, correctly-classified non-retryable failures (`"No GNews credential registered for tenant..."`, `retryable: false`), all from 2026-08-13 — before the credential existed. `shouldAttemptIngestion()` (`connectorHealth.ts`) checks `health.status === 'failing'` and returns `false` unconditionally when true, *before* the activation/credential check that follows it — so once the 20-consecutive absolute ceiling (ADR-0023) trips, the connector can never leave `failing`, because the one thing that would clear the streak (a fresh successful poll) is exactly what this check blocks. Confirmed the credential now genuinely exists (`platform_credentials`, created today) and the connector is activated (`connector_activations.is_active: true`) — neither mattered, because `shouldAttemptIngestion()` never got far enough to check either.

**Confirmed this was never a documented, deliberately-accepted limitation** — ADR-0023's own Context/Consequences frame both its rules purely as detection ("genuinely broken connectors still get caught"), never design what happens after detection; the rate rule already self-heals as its 1-hour window ages, but the absolute ceiling has no equivalent time dimension, since it scans unboundedly backward through history for an unbroken failure streak. Not named in `connector-health-and-error-handling/SKILL.md`'s own "Known gaps" either. **Menno's own explicit direction**, given a structured choice between fixing it properly, manually unsticking the one affected connector, or both: fix it properly.

**Fix: a standard circuit-breaker "half-open" allowance, `PROBE_COOLDOWN_MS` (15 minutes).** Once `failing`, `shouldAttemptIngestion()` now allows exactly one probe attempt through once the most recent attempt (`health.lastAttemptAt`) is older than the cooldown — falling through to the normal activation check, never bypassing it. A probe that succeeds clears the streak via `deriveConnectorHealth()`'s own existing scan-from-newest-until-a-success logic, completely unmodified; a probe that fails just restarts the cooldown (since `lastAttemptAt` updates either way), so a genuinely still-broken connector isn't hammered. `deriveConnectorHealth()`'s own `failing` derivation — the actual 50%/5-attempt-floor/20-consecutive math — is untouched by this fix; only what `shouldAttemptIngestion()` does in reaction to it changed. This meant Story 2.5's own contract (which owns the ceiling rule) needed two new tests (AC5: still blocks within the cooldown; AC6: allows a probe past it, reusing AC3's own `recordSpreadOutFailures()` fixture whose most recent failure already lands 2 hours in the past) — Story 2.3's AC4/AC5 and Story 2.5's own AC1-4 needed zero changes, confirmed by re-reading each against what they actually assert: all check `shouldAttemptIngestion()`/`deriveConnectorHealth()` immediately after a failure, synchronously, well inside any reasonable cooldown window. ADR-0023 gained a matching, dated Clarification section, the same precedent its own 2026-08-12 retryable-exclusion Clarification already established.

- **Full suite at merge:** PASS (29/29 suites, 449/449 tests)

**The admin-side half of Story 6.19, closing it out — the core half (`social-listening-core@aa4f317`, logged above) exposed `bodyMarkdown`; this half actually renders it.** `SocialPostSummary`/`SocialPostFull` (`core-client.ts`) gain the matching `bodyMarkdown: string | null` field. The `react-markdown` dependency (v10.1.0, renders to real React elements — never `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, matching this story's own AC3) is added and used on both post-detail surfaces: `PostsFeedClient.tsx`'s Slideover ("Ingested Article Body") and the standalone `/tenant/posts/:id` route — a `null` `bodyMarkdown` falls back to the existing plain-text `snippet`, never a blank body or a crash. The compact post-card list-view snippet is untouched, confirmed by a dedicated regression test.

**A real environment finding, not a code defect:** `react-markdown` and its whole `unified`/`remark`/`mdast`/`micromark` dependency tree ship ESM-only (`export {`), which this repo's existing Jest config (`ts-jest`, default `transformIgnorePatterns`) couldn't parse — every test importing `PostsFeedClient.tsx` or the `[id]/page.tsx` route failed with `SyntaxError: Unexpected token 'export'`. `jest.config.js`'s `transform` was widened to also cover `.jsx?$` via `ts-jest` (already viable — `tsconfig.json` already had `allowJs: true`/`isolatedModules: true`), and `transformIgnorePatterns` was set to a named allow-list of `react-markdown`'s exact transitive dependency tree (enumerated directly from `node_modules`, ~65 packages), not a blanket "transform all of node_modules" (which would slow every test run). Documented in `post-feed/SKILL.md` as a load-bearing constraint: a future `react-markdown` version bump that adds a new transitive ESM dependency needs that package name added to the list.

**A minimal testability seam, same precedent as Story 6.14's `initialEntries`:** `PostsFeedClient` gains an optional `initialActivePostId` prop, seeding the Slideover's `activePost` state on mount — this repo has no DOM-interaction test runner (`testEnvironment: 'node'`, no jsdom) to actually click a card open, so this seam lets the contract prove the Slideover's real rendered Markdown output via `renderToStaticMarkup()`. Real usage (`page.tsx`) never passes it, confirmed directly.

**Requested directly by Menno** ("what needs to change to enable the feeds to be administered?", then "let's build the new ADR"), picked up immediately after ADR-0057's acceptance. Three new `tenant_admin`-only REST endpoints on `tenantOwnedFeedRouter.ts`: `GET .../activations` (lists every activation for the caller's tenant, any status — `serializeActivation()` recomputes `txtRecordValue` from the stored token, never separately persisted); `PATCH /:id` (`feedUrl` only — a request containing `domain` is a `400`, since domain is the exact claim DNS TXT verification proves); `DELETE /:id` (soft-removal via the new `removeActivation()` store function, transitioning `status` to a new `'removed'` value — migration `0031` widens the `CHECK` constraint via the required `DROP CONSTRAINT`/`ADD CONSTRAINT` shape, since PostgreSQL can't alter one in place; no new `GRANT` needed, `'removed'` is set via `UPDATE`, already granted).

**`connect`/`verify-domain` both gain the `tenant_admin` role check every other tenant-wide connector action in this codebase already has** — closing a real, pre-existing inconsistency found while scoping this story (neither previously checked `identity.role` at all). **`connect` also gains a domain-reuse auto-verify path** (`hasVerifiedDomain()`, new store function): when the caller's tenant already holds a `verified` activation for the exact domain being connected, the new row is created and immediately `markVerified()`-ed server-side, skipping the DNS TXT step entirely — domain ownership is proven once per domain, not once per feed URL. Story 2.11's own contract, which predates this role gate, needed a real, dated update (not a silent rewrite) at every one of its 4 real HTTP call sites against `connect`/`verify-domain` — switched from the default `tenant_user` test identity to an explicit `tenant_admin` header, the exact same behavior otherwise fully unchanged and re-verified passing.

**`TenantOwnedFeedSetup.tsx`'s single-activation state machine is replaced by a real per-tenant feed list.** `page.tsx` now fetches every activation server-side (`listTenantOwnedFeedActivations()`, new), the same "Server Component fetches, Client Component only mutates" pattern `/tenant/users` already established — dropping the old `?activationId=` URL-persistence trick and its `searchParams` prop entirely, since the new list endpoint returns full, real data (including `txtRecordHost`/`txtRecordValue` for any still-`pending` row) on every single page load. Per-row actions, all `tenant_admin`-only: "Verify now" (`pending` only, unchanged `verifyTenantOwnedFeedDomain()` call); "Edit feed URL" (any status, via the shared `Modal` primitive, new `updateTenantOwnedFeedActivation()`); "Remove" (any status, behind `ConfirmModal`, new `removeTenantOwnedFeedActivation()`). A persistent "Connect another feed" action is never gated on the list being empty. A new banner (`tof-banner-inactive`) renders when real feeds exist but the tenant-wide `ActivateDeactivateButton` switch is off, per ADR-0057 Decision §6's own explicit requirement — otherwise a tenant would see "Verified" on every row with no way to understand why nothing is being ingested.

**Stories 6.12's and 6.17's own contracts both needed real, dated corrections — not silent rewrites — for the specific assertions tied to the now-superseded single-activation code shape.** Story 6.12: the `initialActivationId` prop assertion (that prop no longer exists) and the entire AC4 describe block (`?activationId=` URL persistence, retired outright — the mechanism it proved no longer exists by design, the replacement behavior is proven by story-6.20's own contract). Story 6.17: the literal `'Domain verified — this feed is now connected and active.'` unconditional-copy check and the `'if (verified)'`/`'if (activationId)'` branch-marker structural check (neither code shape exists anymore) — replaced with equivalent-intent checks against the new design (the `tof-banner-inactive` banner's own `!isActive` condition; `ActivateDeactivateButton` rendered inside the same `isTenantAdmin &&` block). Every other assertion in both files — `verifyTenantOwnedFeedDomain()`'s own core-client/proxy behavior, the "not yet verified"/"try again" retry copy, the never-disabled "Verify now" button, `ActivateDeactivateButton`'s own props — needed no change and was re-confirmed passing unmodified.

- **Full suite at merge:** PASS (60/62 suites, 485/490 tests — the two failing suites, story-2.7.gnews-connector and story-3.9.author-follower-count-at-publish, both fail on the same real, already-documented GNews free-tier daily-quota exhaustion (`403`) from this session's own heavy real-API usage — confirmed directly via the actual thrown error and `docs/environment-gotchas.md`'s own matching precedent, not a regression; re-run in isolation, both fail identically, ruling out cross-suite interference)

**Closes the gap `ingestion-events/SKILL.md`'s own "Known gaps" section already named directly: `publishEvent()` had no real caller anywhere in the actual ingestion pipeline.** Found live while scoping Story 2.13 (Wikipedia connector, separately paused pending this ADR) — Menno flagged it directly. `ADR-0058` (drafted, revised at review, and accepted the same session) decides per-connector `SocialPostIngestedEvent` publishing and shared `runIngestionAttempt()`-level `ConnectorHealthChangedEvent` publishing; both best-effort, matching `enrichPost()`'s own never-blocks-ingestion precedent (ADR-0038).

**New `listActiveWatchlistsForTenant()` (`watchlistStore.ts`) iterates the tenant's own users and reuses the existing, unmodified `listWatchlists()` once per user — no new Postgres role, no RLS policy change.** A real technical obstacle surfaced during implementation, not assumed away: `watchlists`' own RLS policy (`migrations/0025`) requires `app.user_id` to match a real owner, with no "see every user" fallback, and this project's own `tenant_deletion_role` precedent — which already holds a `GRANT SELECT, DELETE ON watchlists` — turned out not to bypass it either (it relies on `ON DELETE CASCADE` via `users`, not a direct query). Given watchlists are deliberately private per-user (ADR-0044 §5c), Menno's own explicit direction was not to mint a new standing elevated-privilege credential to save a small, poll-batch-scoped loop.

**New `src/events/publishSocialPostIngestedEvents.ts` deliberately always uses the fallback AST evaluator (`matchesAst`), never `resolveWatchlistAstDispatch()`'s `'native'` shortcut — a real correctness bug caught by this story's own AC3 contract, not by inspection.** GNews already declares real `supportedQueryFeatures` (Story 2.7), so dispatch would resolve `'native'` for any TERM-based watchlist — but `'native'` mode's own contract assumes the connector's actual outbound fetch was parameterized by that exact watchlist's query, which none of the three real connectors do (each polls one fixed query/feed set). Trusting it would have matched every TERM-capable watchlist unconditionally, regardless of real post content. `docs/adr/0058-...md`'s own Amendment Log and `ingestion-events/SKILL.md`'s Load-bearing constraints both document this correction directly, per this project's "historical record, append corrections" convention.

**Each connector's own `ingestXItems()` function loads its tenant's active, platform-filtered watchlists exactly once per poll batch** (Decision §6's own named N+1 safeguard, proven by AC4's spy-count assertion) **and publishes only after `insertSocialPost()`'s own promise has resolved** — proven for real by AC5, whose mocked `publishEvent()` performs an independent read of `social_posts` at the moment of publish and confirms the row is already visible. `runIngestionAttempt()` takes a health snapshot before `startIngestionRun()` and another after `completeIngestionRun()`/each terminal `return`, publishing exactly one `ConnectorHealthChangedEvent` only when status actually changed — proven end-to-end against the real Service Bus topic (AC6: a fresh tenant's first-ever attempt flips `disconnected` → `healthy`; a second, unchanged attempt publishes nothing).

- **Full suite at merge:** PASS (61/63 suites, 499/504 tests — the two failing suites, story-2.7.gnews-connector and story-3.9.author-follower-count-at-publish, are the same real, already-documented GNews free-tier daily-quota exhaustion (`403`) confirmed environmental during Story 5.19 earlier this same session, not a regression)

**Closes a real, confirmed drafting gap named in Story 2.13's own epic entry**: ADR-0042 was Accepted 2026-08-08 but never actually built — `wikipediaConnector.ts` (the registered connector object, `fetchWikipediaSearch()`/`fetchWikipediaRecentChanges()`/`fetchWikipediaRevision()`) had already been drafted and verified live against the real MediaWiki Action API earlier this session, ahead of any contract test — a real, self-acknowledged deviation from this project's own contract-first order, corrected starting with this pass by writing the Story 2.13 contract first, against the already-drafted implementation, rather than continuing to build ahead of it.

**Two-phase `pollWikipedia()` (new `pollWikipedia.ts`)**: discovery (`fetchWikipediaSearch()`, ingesting a genuinely new `pageid`'s current revision) and re-poll (`fetchWikipediaRecentChanges()` per already-tracked article's own current title, via the new `listAuthorsByPlatform()` in `authorStore.ts` — this connector's own "already discovered" membership check, since no separate tracking table exists). `ingestWikipediaRevisions()` follows the identical per-post shape every other real connector's own `ingestXItems()` already establishes: dedup via `findSocialPostByExternalId()`, `upsertAuthor()` keyed by `pageid` (`displayName` = the article's current title, `followerCount` left unpopulated — ADR-0042 Decision §3's own organization-as-Author instance), `htmlToMarkdown()` body conversion, best-effort `enrichPost()`, `insertSocialPost()` with `raw_payload.url` set to the specific revision's own `?oldid=` permalink (ADR-0042 Decision §4). **Adopts Story 5.19's own event-publishing wiring as part of this connector's original build (ADR-0058 Decision §5, not a separate follow-up)** — watchlists loaded once per poll batch, `publishSocialPostIngestedEvents()` called post-commit per successfully-inserted revision.

**`getRateLimitConfig()` ships an explicitly-labeled conservative placeholder** (60 requests/60s) — Wikimedia's own rate-limits policy was referenced but not fetched to a confirmed exact ceiling at ADR-0042 drafting time, the same honest treatment ADR-0024 gave Newswire's own unpublished limit. **`supportedQueryFeatures` is declared empty** — CirrusSearch's exact native-query surface through the standard `search` endpoint was never confirmed to this project's own primary-source bar; watchlist matching always falls back to whole-article post-fetch matching. **No `rcstart` narrowing on `recentchanges`** — correctness (no duplicate rows) is guaranteed entirely by the shared dedup check, not by narrowing the API call itself; a real, named inefficiency, not a correctness gap (see `wikipedia-connector/SKILL.md`'s own Load-bearing constraints).

**Story 1.13's own connector-count assertion needed a real, dated update, not a silent rewrite**: `bootstrapConnectors()` now registers a fourth real connector, so `story-1.13...contract.test.ts`'s hardcoded `[GNEWS, NEWSWIRE, TENANT_OWNED_FEED]` exact-equality check would otherwise fail — extended to include `WIKIPEDIA_PROVIDER_ID` with a dated note, per this project's own "Regression, not rewrite" convention; every other assertion in that file targets a specific connector by id and was unaffected.

**A real, confirmed documentation bug in Story 2.13's own epic entry was corrected in the same pass** (found and flagged before this build began): its own "Built 2026-08-12" note verbatim described Story 2.11's tenant-owned-feed work, not Wikipedia — no contract, no `src/connectors/wikipedia/` directory, and no Implementation Log entry existed for Story 2.13 at the time it was written. Removed and replaced with a dated Documentation Steward correction note; the story's own `**Built:**` field is updated below to the real commit this entry names.

## 2026-08-18 — Story 2.14 — social-listening-core@c802b64

- **Full commit:** `c802b64ab2160d8a640ce7249655554201129630`
- **Repo:** social-listening-core
- **Story / ADR:** 2.14 / ADR-0042 §5 (already-Accepted; no new decision — resolves the ADR's own named implementation-time task)
- **Contract:** social-listening-core/contracts/epic-2/story-2.14.wikipedia-watchlist-driven-discovery.contract.test.ts (new, 8/8)

- **Files touched:** docs/implementation-plan.md, docs/user-stories/README.md, docs/user-stories/epic-2-ingestion-connectors-and-rate-limits.md, social-listening-core/.claude/skills/wikipedia-connector/SKILL.md, social-listening-core/contracts/epic-2/story-2.14.wikipedia-watchlist-driven-discovery.contract.test.ts, social-listening-core/src/connectors/wikipedia/pollWikipedia.ts
- **Full suite at merge:** PASS (62/64 suites, 507/512 tests — the two failing suites, story-2.7.gnews-connector and story-3.9.author-follower-count-at-publish, are the same real, already-documented GNews free-tier daily-quota exhaustion (`403`) named in `docs/environment-gotchas.md` and confirmed environmental during Stories 5.19/2.13 earlier this same series, not a regression; confirmed with Menno before proceeding)

**Found live while Menno activated the Wikipedia connector in the Tenant Admin UI**: no mechanism, UI or backend, let a tenant tell the connector which article/topic to track. Confirmed directly against the real code: `pollWikipedia(tenantId, query = DEFAULT_QUERY)` defaulted `query` to the module-level literal `'Anthropic'`, and `bootstrapConnectors.ts`'s own real production call site (`live-ingestion-polling-scheduler`) called `pollWikipedia(tenantId)` with no second argument at all — every tenant's every poll cycle searched the same shared literal, regardless of what that tenant actually watched for. This was already a named, documented gap in Story 2.13's own SKILL.md ("No real per-watchlist discovery query — shared gap with GNews"), never resolved. ADR-0042 §5 itself had already named the fix as implementation-time work ("Exact AST-to-CirrusSearch-syntax translation is an implementation-time task, not fixed by this ADR"), so no new ADR was drafted — the same "resolve an already-Accepted ADR's own named open item directly" precedent Stories 5.8/5.11/5.17 established.

**A real, discovered constraint shaped the design, not a fresh choice**: Story 2.13's own passing contract calls `pollWikipedia(tenantId, TEST_ARTICLE)` with an explicit query — removing the parameter would have rewritten a passing contract, forbidden by this project's own "regression, not rewrite" hard rule. `query` is therefore kept as an *optional* override (unchanged behavior when supplied); only the **omitted-query path** — the one real production call site has always taken — now derives discovery from the tenant's own active watchlists targeting `wikipedia` instead of falling back to any shared literal. A new, unexported `buildDiscoveryQueries()` helper builds one query per `keyword`/`hashtag`/`account`-typed watchlist from its own `terms` (space-joined); `boolean`-typed watchlists are deliberately skipped for discovery (real AST-to-CirrusSearch operator translation remains ADR-0042's own unverified open question) but stay fully evaluated for event-matching once content is discovered another way. A tenant with zero matching watchlists now performs zero discovery searches, rather than silently searching someone else's topic.

**GNews's identical, separately-tracked `DEFAULT_QUERY` gap (`pollGNewsSearch.ts`) is untouched by this story** — named explicitly as out of scope in Story 2.14's own text, not silently left unmentioned.

## 2026-08-18, later the same day — Story 2.16 — social-listening-core@3fedac3

- **Full commit:** `3fedac3ce1436b0e3384bd1d4acfa3d56c35fcdb`
- **Repo:** social-listening-core
- **Story / ADR:** 2.16 / No new ADR — corrects an implementation gap against ADR-0038's already-Accepted "enrichment is best-effort, never a hard dependency of ingestion succeeding" text, the same category Story 2.12 already used for `deriveConnectorHealth()`
- **Contract:** social-listening-core/contracts/epic-2/story-2.16.azure-ai-language-classified-document-error.contract.test.ts (new, 3/3)
- **SKILL.md:** social-listening-core/.claude/skills/azure-ai-language-connector/SKILL.md (updated)
- **Files touched:** docs/implementation-plan.md, docs/user-stories/README.md, docs/user-stories/epic-2-ingestion-connectors-and-rate-limits.md, social-listening-core/.claude/skills/azure-ai-language-connector/SKILL.md, social-listening-core/contracts/epic-2/story-2.16.azure-ai-language-classified-document-error.contract.test.ts, social-listening-core/src/connectors/azureAiLanguage/azureAiLanguageConnector.ts
- **Full suite at merge:** PASS (65/65 suites, 515/515 tests)

**Found live while investigating why Wikipedia watchlists (Stories 2.13/2.14) returned zero ingested posts despite the connector being activated and correctly discovering real articles.** Direct inspection of the persistent local dev database (`social_listening_dev`, port 5435) confirmed every real `ingestion_runs` row for `wikipedia` was permanently stuck at `status: 'running'`, `posts_ingested: 0` — no Wikipedia post had ever reached `social_posts`, while GNews/Newswire/tenant-owned-feed all had real posts. Watchlist wiring itself was confirmed correct (all three tenant watchlists targeted `wikipedia`, the connector was activated, discovery had found four real articles) — the gap was downstream, in enrichment.

**Reproduced directly against the real Azure AI Language endpoint**, not assumed: sending the real ~37KB Markdown body a large Wikipedia article (`htmlToMarkdown()`'s own real output for "Microsoft") produces, `azureAiLanguageConnector.analyze()` threw a raw, unclassified `TypeError` — `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'entities')`. Captured the exact real Azure response body that causes it: a genuine HTTP **200** (`response.ok` true — the connector's own existing `!response.ok` branch never fires) with `results.documents` empty and the real reason in `results.errors` instead — `"A document within the request was too large to be processed. Document contains 40000 text elements. Limit document size to: 5120 text elements."` `analyze()` unconditionally indexed `results.documents[0]` on all four capability calls with no check. `enrichPost.ts`'s own `tryProvider()` does catch a non-`ClassifiableError` ("a programming error must not break ingestion either") and silently fails over to Azure OpenAI — so this never failed ingestion outright, but it has been silently failing Azure AI Language enrichment for every oversized document (every real Wikipedia post processed so far) with zero logging, until this session's direct investigation traced it back.

**Fix**: a new `firstDocument()` helper checks each of the four capability responses for a present document before any field is read off it, throwing `ClassifiableError('network', ...)` — the same `ErrorKind` this connector's own adjacent generic-4xx branch already uses (confirmed via direct grep as this project's identical convention across every connector — `gnewsConnector.ts`/`newswireConnector.ts`/`azureOpenAiConnector.ts`/`tenantOwnedFeedConnector.ts`/`wikipediaConnector.ts`), not a new connector-specific `ErrorKind` — with the real Azure error detail folded into the thrown message. **A real, pre-existing, adjacent inaccuracy corrected in the same pass**: that branch's own doc comment claimed `'network'` was "the closest existing non-retryable bucket" — factually wrong, `'network'` is in `errorClassification.ts`'s own `RETRYABLE_KINDS` set; corrected to describe the actual reason (project-wide convention, not retry semantics), with no behavior change.

**Named, not fixed here** (`azure-ai-language-connector/SKILL.md`'s own new "Known gaps" section): the underlying size mismatch itself — Wikipedia's full-article enrichment input (~37KB) will still always exceed Azure AI Language's real 5,120-text-element limit and fail over to Azure OpenAI every time; capping/truncating the enrichment *input* text before `enrichPost()` is a real, separate, not-yet-scoped follow-on. No `fetch()` timeout exists anywhere in this connector or the Wikipedia connector either — a broader hardening gap, also not this story's scope. The real, currently-orphaned `wikipedia` `ingestion_runs` rows already stuck at `'running'` in the dev database are an operational data-cleanup item, not touched by this code change.

## 2026-08-18, later still the same day — Story 2.15 — social-listening-core@50a5914

- **Full commit:** `50a5914641dcd9fc03d424f0994555b6389d8a0b`
- **Repo:** social-listening-core
- **Story / ADR:** 2.15 / ADR-0059 (Accepted 2026-08-18) — Facebook connector, tenant's own connected Page, posts only, Tier 3 credential
- **Contract:** social-listening-core/contracts/epic-2/story-2.15.facebook-connector.contract.test.ts (new, 13/13)
- **SKILL.md:** social-listening-core/.claude/skills/facebook-connector/SKILL.md (new); social-listening-core/.claude/skills/connector-health-and-error-handling/SKILL.md (updated)
- **Files touched:** docs/environment-gotchas.md, docs/implementation-plan.md, docs/user-stories/README.md, docs/user-stories/epic-2-ingestion-connectors-and-rate-limits.md, social-listening-core/.claude/skills/connector-health-and-error-handling/SKILL.md, social-listening-core/.claude/skills/facebook-connector/SKILL.md, social-listening-core/contracts/epic-1/story-1.13.live-ingestion-polling-scheduler.contract.test.ts, social-listening-core/contracts/epic-2/story-2.15.facebook-connector.contract.test.ts, social-listening-core/migrations/0032_add_ingestion_runs_credential_failure_flag.sql, social-listening-core/src/connectors/bootstrapConnectors.ts, social-listening-core/src/connectors/connectorHealth.ts, social-listening-core/src/connectors/facebook/facebookConnector.ts, social-listening-core/src/connectors/facebook/pollFacebook.ts, social-listening-core/src/http/versions/v1/connectorsRouter.ts, social-listening-core/src/http/versions/v1/facebookOAuthRouter.ts, social-listening-core/src/http/versions/v1/router.ts, social-listening-core/src/ingestion/ingestionRunStore.ts, social-listening-core/src/ingestion/runIngestionAttempt.ts
- **Full suite at merge:** PASS (67/67 suites, 531/531 tests, `--runInBand` — see this session's own new environment-gotchas.md entry: the full suite showed real, non-deterministic resource-contention failures under load in earlier runs this same session, reproducing cleanly neither the failure nor a fixed cause; every specific failing suite passed cleanly in isolation both times, confirming no real regression)

**This project's first `authMode: 'oauth'` connector and first Tier-3-only connector.** ADR-0059's own real, primary-source-verified finding: genuine public-content social listening is not buildable against Meta's current Graph API at all (no keyword-search/discovery surface exists) — this connector is narrowed to a tenant's own connected Facebook Page's own published posts only, never comments, never public content the tenant doesn't own. `facebookConnector.ts`/`pollFacebook.ts` follow the established connector shape (organization-as-Author, a fourth real instance of ADR-0004's already-generalized clause, argued explicitly per ADR-0059 Decision §5; `supportedQueryFeatures` empty; a conservative, explicitly-labeled `getRateLimitConfig()` placeholder, since ADR-0059's own confirmed real ceiling — 4,800× the Page's own Engaged Users — is per-Page dynamic and `ProviderConnector.getRateLimitConfig()` is synchronous/zero-arg). `facebookOAuthRouter.ts` is the dedicated backend half of the real OAuth flow: a real authorization-code exchange → long-lived user token → `/me/accounts`, with every Page access token cached server-side only (an in-process, short-TTL `Map` keyed by a single-use `sessionToken`, reusing `connectorHealthCache.ts`'s own established pattern for a genuinely different purpose) — never round-tripped through the client (ADR-0036's "no bearer token in browser JS" posture, applied to Meta's own tokens). The generic `POST /:platformId/connect` now rejects `authMode: 'oauth'` platforms outright — the actual mechanism enforcing ADR-0059 Decision §4's "no Tier 2 path at all" rule, since nothing else in this project could.

**`ConnectorHealth` gains a new `reconnect_required` status** (migration `0032`, a new `ingestion_runs.is_credential_failure` column, computed once at `runIngestionAttempt()`'s own two `completeIngestionRun({status:'failed'})` call sites via the already-existing `isCredentialError()`) — distinct from `failing` because a revoked OAuth grant will not self-heal no matter how many times it's retried, the silent-failure problem ADR-0059 Decision §4 named directly.

**Verified against real, live infrastructure this session, not assumed**: a real Meta Developer App (App ID `427795247628927`) and a real test Page — confirmed directly against the live Graph API (a real `/me?fields=id,name,category` call and a real `/feed` fetch returning a real post) before building against it. `.env` gained `FACEBOOK_APP_ID`/`FACEBOOK_APP_SECRET`/`FACEBOOK_TEST_PAGE_ID`/`FACEBOOK_TEST_PAGE_ACCESS_TOKEN`, the same "real, dedicated test-fixture credential" pattern GNews/Azure AI Language/Azure OpenAI already established.

**Found and fixed a real cross-component regression along the way, not folded in silently — its own healing pass, following `heal-contract-failure`'s full walk, not an ad hoc patch:**
- `bootstrapConnectors()`'s first draft registered `facebookConnector` with no `.poll`/`.pollCadenceMs` wrapper at all, reasoning that the scheduler's own `if (connector.poll && ...)` guard would safely skip it. Story 1.13's own AC1 already asserts, as a real, pre-existing invariant, that *every* registered poll-mode connector has both — violated regardless of whether the scheduler could ever reach it. Fixed: Facebook now gets a real wrapper whose `.poll()` throws a clear, named error if ever actually invoked (structurally unreachable today, since `shouldAttemptIngestion()`'s own tenant-wide-only eligibility check always fails first for a Tier-3-only connector) — honestly documented as unreachable, not silently faked as working. Story 1.13's own hardcoded 4-connector `providerIds` list was also extended to 5, with a dated note matching that file's own already-established precedent for the identical Wikipedia addition.
- `deriveConnectorHealth()`'s first draft of `reconnect_required` short-circuited with an early return *before* the existing `consecutiveFailures`-counting loop ran at all, hardcoding it to `0`. Story 1.13's own GNews credential-failure test (a missing credential is *also* `http_401`, a credential-class error) proved this broke real, already-established behavior — `consecutiveFailures` is supposed to still increment normally for that exact case. Fixed: the new status now overrides only the final computed label, after the complete existing derivation (recentFailures/recentSuccesses/consecutiveFailures/lastSuccessfulFetchAt) runs entirely unchanged.

**Real Tier-3 poll scheduling is not built** — `pollFacebook(tenantId, userId)` works correctly when called directly (proven by this story's own contract) but nothing in the real running server calls it on a schedule yet; ADR-0052 Decision §6 already named this exact gap before this connector existed. Named honestly in this connector's own SKILL.md Known gaps, not silently assumed solved.

**A real, honest scope limitation, named up front, not discovered later:** `enrichPost.ts` tries Azure AI Language first (Menno's own confirmed intentional default, 2026-08-12) and it succeeds for most ordinary-length content — Azure AI Language's four capabilities have no summarization output of their own (real Azure AI Language document summarization is a separate, asynchronous endpoint, verified this session against current Microsoft Learn docs: up to 125,000 characters, but a genuinely different, poll-based call shape — a materially heavier build, tracked as a real follow-up per Menno's own "see how it works, then decide" direction, not built here). `summary` is therefore populated only for posts that actually reach Azure OpenAI, not universally.

**Implementation**: `AnalyzeResult` (`types.ts`) gains an optional `summary?: string`, additive per every prior widening of this interface. `ENRICHMENT_SCHEMA` gains a `summary` property (added to `required`, since Azure OpenAI's `strict: true` structured-output mode requires every schema property be listed as required). The system prompt is extended to request a concise, neutral summary as part of the same self-review pass Story 2.9 already established, folded into the existing instruction rather than a separate block. `summary` round-trips through `insertSocialPost()` into `SocialPost.enrichment.summary` unchanged — no migration, no new column, since `enrichment` is already an unfiltered JSONB blob (same storage path Story 2.8 AC4 already proved). A tenant served by Azure AI Language instead (both providers connected/active, the existing fixed-order default) has `enrichment.summary` absent, not a placeholder — proven directly, not assumed.

## 2026-08-18, later the same day — Story 6.25 — social-listening-admin@6550716

- **Full commit:** `65507164bac4ef7fdfb48d82627a940670b39155`
- **Repo:** social-listening-admin

- **Contract:** social-listening-admin/contracts/epic-6/story-6.25.post-feed-newest-first.contract.test.ts (new, 5/5)
- **SKILL.md:** social-listening-admin/.claude/skills/post-feed/SKILL.md (updated)
- **Files touched:** docs/implementation-plan.md, docs/user-stories/README.md, docs/user-stories/epic-6-tenant-admin-ui.md, social-listening-admin/.claude/skills/post-feed/SKILL.md, social-listening-admin/contracts/epic-6/story-6.25.post-feed-newest-first.contract.test.ts, social-listening-admin/src/app/tenant/posts/page.tsx
- **Full suite at merge:** PASS (33/33 suites, 493/493 tests)

**Requested directly by Menno, 2026-08-18.** Confirmed directly against the real backend: `GET /v1/posts` (`socialPostStore.ts`'s `queryFirstPage`/`queryAfterCursor`) orders every page `ORDER BY seq ASC` — `seq` is a monotonic, insertion-ordered identity column (ADR-0011/Story 3.4), so the first page returned is the oldest-ingested posts. `fetchAllPosts()` (Story 6.18) already pages through the tenant's entire post set into memory before `PostsFeedClient` renders/filters anything, and handed that array through unmodified — oldest-ingested-first.

**A deliberately minimal fix, not a pagination redesign**: rather than reversing the backend's own `ORDER BY seq ASC` to `DESC` (which would flip the cursor's own keyset comparison direction — a real, non-trivial change to ADR-0011's already-contract-verified mechanism), `fetchAllPosts()` reverses the already-fully-fetched, in-memory array once, client-side, after paging completes. The backend's pagination mechanism, cursor encoding, and `seq ASC` ordering are all completely unchanged. `PostsFeedClient.tsx` has no `.sort()` of its own (confirmed directly, not assumed) — order is preserved end to end through its existing `.filter()`/`.slice()` derivation, proven by a test showing a filtered/searched result set keeps the same relative newest-first order as the input.
